# Proposed OEIS comments: A264132 to A269273

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A264132

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264132>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [566](../papers/0501-1000/0566-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) -a(n-2) +15*a(n-4) -60*a(n-5) +15*a(n-6) -15*a(n-8) +60*a(n-9) -15*a(n-10) +a(n-12) -4*a(n-13) +a(n-14)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264142

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264142>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2880](../papers/2501-3000/2880-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrences for columns k = 1, 2 and 3 are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264185

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264185>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8837](../papers/8501-9000/8837-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*a(n-1) for n>4.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 05 2019: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264268

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264268>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [271](../papers/0001-0500/0271-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 52 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 52, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264509

What the entry records as unsettled:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 69, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A264572

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A264572>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [260](../papers/0001-0500/0260-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 1 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

Paper: [2463](../papers/2001-2500/2463-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 23*a(n-1) - 106*a(n-2) - 724*a(n-3) + 4128*a(n-4) + 9216*a(n-5) - 20736*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(1 - 7*x - 37*x^2 + 270*x^3 + 540*x^4 - 1296*x^5) / ((1 + 4*x)*(1 - 9*x)*(1 - 12*x + 16*x^2)*(1 - 6*x - 36*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 11 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 401 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268554

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268554>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [8590](../papers/8501-9000/8590-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n^3*(2*n-1)*a(n) -6*(4*n-1)*(3*n-1)*(3*n-2)*(4*n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 10 2016
> For integer r and positive integer s, define sequences {u(n) : n >= 0} and {v(n) : n >= 0} by setting u(n) = [x^(s*n)] F(x)^(r*n) and v(n) = [x^(s*n)] E(x)^(r*n). We conjecture that both u(n) and v(n) satisfy the above supercongruences. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 45 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
